```
    15→def _subjective_threshold(state: dict[str, Any], *, default: float = 95.0) -> float:
    16→    config = state.get("config", {})
    17→    raw_target = default
    18→    if isinstance(config, dict):
    19→        raw_target = config.get("target_strict_score", default)
    20→    try:
    21→        value = float(raw_target)
    22→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
    23→        value = default
    24→    return max(0.0, min(100.0, value))
    25→
    26→
    27→@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
    28→class OpenPlanQueuePolicy:
    29→    tier: int | None = None
    30→    count: int | None = None
    31→    scan_path: str | None = None
    32→    include_subjective: bool = True
    33→    no_tier_fallback: bool = True
    34→
    35→
    36→def build_open_plan_queue(
    37→    state: dict[str, Any],
    38→    policy: OpenPlanQueuePolicy | None = None,
    39→) -> WorkQueueResult:
    40→    """Build one open-status queue with consistent planning policy defaults."""
    41→    policy = policy or OpenPlanQueuePolicy()
    42→    resolved_scan_path: str | None
    43→    if isinstance(policy.scan_path, str):
    44→        resolved_scan_path = policy.scan_path
    45→    else:
    46→        raw_state_path = state.get("scan_path")
    47→        resolved_scan_path = raw_state_path if isinstance(raw_state_path, str) else None
    48→    return build_work_queue(
    49→        state,
    50→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
    51→            tier=policy.tier,
    52→            count=policy.count,
    53→            scan_path=resolved_scan_path,
    54→            status="open",
    55→            include_subjective=policy.include_subjective,
    56→            subjective_threshold=_subjective_threshold(state),
    57→            no_tier_fallback=policy.no_tier_fallback,
    58→        ),
    59→    )
```

```
1→"""String-aware TypeScript character scanner."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Generator
     6→
     7→
     8→def scan_code(
     9→    text: str, start: int = 0, end: int | None = None
    10→) -> Generator[tuple[int, str, bool], None, None]:
    11→    """Yield ``(index, char, in_string)`` tuples while handling escapes."""
    12→    i = start
    13→    limit = end if end is not None else len(text)
    14→    in_str = None
    15→    while i < limit:
    16→        ch = text[i]
    17→        if in_str:
    18→            if ch == "\\" and i + 1 < limit:
    19→                yield (i, ch, True)
    20→                i += 1
    21→                yield (i, text[i], True)
    22→                i += 1
    23→                continue
    24→            if ch == in_str:
    25→                in_str = None
    26→            yield (i, ch, in_str is not None)
    27→        else:
    28→            if ch in ("'", '"', "`"):
    29→                in_str = ch
    30→                yield (i, ch, True)
    31→            else:
    32→                yield (i, ch, False)
    33→        i += 1
    34→
    35→
```

```
     7→     7→- OBSERVE: lightweight — write an analysis of themes/root causes (100 char min)
     8→     8→- REFLECT: compare current findings against completed work (recurring patterns)

    45→    45→def _short_id(fid: str) -> str:
    46→    46→    """Extract the 8-char hash suffix from a finding ID for compact display.
    47→    47→
    48→    48→    Finding IDs look like ``review::.::holistic::dim::identifier::abcdef12``.
    49→    49→    Commands accept the hash suffix as a shorthand for the full ID.
    50→    50→    """
    51→    51→    if "::" in fid:
    52→    52→        suffix = fid.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
    53→    53→        if len(suffix) >= 8:
    54→    54→            return suffix[:8]
    55→    55→    return fid
    56→    56→
    57→    57→
    58→    58→def _synthesis_coverage(plan: dict) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
    59→    59→    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for synthesis progress."""
    60→    60→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    61→    61→    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
    62→    62→    for c in clusters.values():
    63→    63→        all_cluster_ids.update(c.get("finding_ids", []))
    64→    64→    queue_ids = [fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", []) if fid != SYNTHESIS_ID]
    65→    65→    organized = sum(1 for fid in queue_ids if fid in all_cluster_ids)
    66→    66→    return organized, len(queue_ids), clusters
    67→    67→
    68→    68→
    69→    69→def _unenriched_clusters(plan: dict) -> list[tuple[str, list[str]]]:
    70→    70→    """Return clusters with findings that are missing required enrichment.
    71→    71→
    72→    72→    Returns list of (cluster_name, list_of_missing_fields).
    73→    73→    Only checks non-auto clusters (manual clusters the agent creates).
    74→    74→    """
    75→    75→    gaps: list[tuple[str, list[str]]] = []
    76→    76→    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
    77→    77→        if not cluster.get("finding_ids"):
    78→    78→            continue
    79→    79→        if cluster.get("auto"):
    80→    80→            continue
    81→    81→        missing: list[str] = []
    82→    82→        if not cluster.get("description"):
    83→    83→            missing.append("description")
    84→    84→        if not cluster.get("action_steps"):
    85→    85→            missing.append("action_steps")
    86→    86→        if missing:

    88→    88→    return gaps
    89→    89→
    90→    90→
    91→    91→def _manual_clusters_with_findings(plan: dict) -> list[str]:

    99→    99→def _print_stage_progress(stages: dict, plan: dict | None = None) -> None:
   100→   100→    """Print the 4-stage progress indicator."""
   101→   101→    print(colorize("  Stages:", "dim"))
   102→   102→    for stage_name, label in SYNTHESIS_STAGE_LABELS:

   107→   107→        else:

   109→   109→
   110→   110→    # Show enrichment gaps when in the organize stage
   111→   111→    if plan and "reflect" in stages and "organize" not in stages:
   112→   112→        gaps = _unenriched_clusters(plan)
   113→   113→        manual = _manual_clusters_with_findings(plan)
   114→   114→        if not manual:

   116→   116→        elif gaps:

   126→   126→

   128→   128→def _print_progress(plan: dict, open_findings: dict) -> None:
   129→   129→    """Show cluster state and unclustered findings."""
   130→   130→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   131→   131→    # Only show clusters that actually have findings (hide empty/stale ones)
   132→   132→    active_clusters = {

   134→   134→        if c.get("finding_ids")

   138→   138→        for name, cluster in active_clusters.items():
   139→   139→            count = len(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   140→   140→            desc = cluster.get("description") or ""

   146→   146→            if desc:

   148→   148→            else:
   149→   149→                tags.append("no desc")
   150→   150→            if steps:
   151→   151→                tags.append(f"{len(steps)} steps")
   152→   152→            else:
   153→   153→                if not auto:
   154→   154→                    tags.append("no steps")
   155→   155→            tag_str = f" [{', '.join(tags)}]"
   156→   156→            desc_str = f" \u2014 {desc}" if desc else ""
   157→   157→            print(f"    {name}: {count} items{tag_str}{desc_str}")
   158→   158→
   159→   159→    all_clustered: set[str] = set()
   160→   160→    for c in clusters.values():
   161→   161→        all_clustered.update(c.get("finding_ids", []))
   162→   162→    unclustered = [fid for fid in open_findings if fid not in all_clustered]
   163→   163→    if unclustered:

   170→   170→        if len(unclustered) > 10:

   172→   172→    elif open_findings:
   173→   173→        organized, total, _ = _synthesis_coverage(plan)
   174→   174→        print(colorize(f"\n  All {organized}/{total} findings are in clusters.", "green"))
   175→   175→

   177→   177→def _apply_completion(args: argparse.Namespace, plan: dict, strategy: str) -> None:
   178→   178→    """Shared completion logic: update meta, remove synthesis::pending, save."""
   179→   179→    organized, total, clusters = _synthesis_coverage(plan)

   182→   182→    if SYNTHESIS_ID in order:
```

Here is the analysis of each file:

---

## File-by-File Analysis

### 1. `desloppify/app/commands/fix/review_flow.py`
**What changed:** The large `_cmd_fix_review` function was decomposed — its three major output blocks (integrity hints, dimension prompts, lang guide, agent plan) were extracted into four standalone private helper functions (`_print_subjective_integrity_refresh_hints`, `_print_dimension_prompts`, `_print_lang_guide`, `_print_review_agent_plan`). The main function now calls these helpers.
**Net lines:** +45 added, -74 removed (net -29)
**Category: genuine refactor** — pure extraction with no behavior change; the logic is identical, just reorganized.

---

### 2. `desloppify/app/commands/next.py`
**What changed:** Several responsibilities extracted into named helpers (`_resolve_cluster_focus`, `_build_next_payload`, `_emit_requested_output`, `_plan_queue_context`, `_merge_potentials_safe`), plus two genuine feature additions: passing `plan_start_strict`/`queue_total` to the render layer, and a new `cluster_filter` argument to `render_terminal_items`. Deferred imports are hoisted to the top.
**Net lines:** +95 added, -34 removed (net +61)
**Category: feature addition** — the refactor is present but the primary driver is new lifecycle-display behavior (plan-start score tracking, queue progress), which required new wiring.

---

### 3. `desloppify/app/commands/next_parts/render.py`
**What changed:** `show_empty_queue` gains a before/after plan-start score comparison display when clearing the queue. `render_followup_nudges` gains a subjective-bottleneck banner, a rescan-after-structural-work tip, and plan-start/queue-remaining context lines. A new `_render_compact_item` helper and cluster drill-in rendering mode are added. `--attest` flag in resolution hints is replaced with `--confirm`.
**Net lines:** +80 added, -12 removed (net +68)
**Category: feature addition** — substantial new terminal output behavior (lifecycle progress display, bottleneck diagnosis, compact cluster items).

---

### 4. `desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py`
**What changed:** The large `cmd_plan_queue` function was decomposed — plan context resolution, header printing, display item slicing, and row building were each extracted into `_resolve_plan_context`, `_print_queue_header`, `_queue_display_items`, `_build_rows`. The skipped-item display logic inside the header was also inverted for early-return clarity.
**Net lines:** +60 added, -60 removed (net 0)
**Category: genuine refactor** — essentially a zero-net-change decomposition with no new behavior.

---

### 5. `desloppify/app/commands/resolve/render.py`
**What changed:** Inside `_print_score_movement`, three logical blocks (snapshot validation, strict-gap wontfix note, post-resolve guidance) were extracted into `_score_snapshot_or_warn`, `_print_strict_gap_note`, and `_print_post_resolve_guidance`. A deferred import for `_print_strict_target_nudge` is hoisted to top-of-file.
**Net lines:** +63 added, -56 removed (net +7)
**Category: genuine refactor** — pure decomposition, no new output logic introduced.

---

### 6. `desloppify/app/commands/review/entrypoint.py`
**What changed:** `cmd_review` was decomposed into `_require_lang_or_exit`, `_mode_flags` (extracts mode-flag parsing), `_validate_mode_selection_or_exit`, and `_run_review_mode`. One behavior change: `external_submit` now also calls `review_rerun_preflight` (previously it did not). The `--merge` import is hoisted.
**Net lines:** +70 added, -40 removed (net +30)
**Category: genuine refactor** with one incidental behavior fix (`external_submit` now goes through preflight).

---

### 7. `desloppify/app/commands/review/preflight.py`
**What changed:** The gating logic in `review_rerun_preflight` now blocks on both objective AND subjective open queue items (previously only objective). Several large inline blocks are extracted into `_normalize_dimension_key`, `_count_open_subjective_queue_items`, `_blocking_scored_dimensions`, `_objective_and_subjective_backlog`, and `_print_backlog_blocked_message`. The blocked message is now more detailed.
**Net lines:** +143 added, -73 removed (net +70)
**Category: feature addition** — genuinely new gating behavior (subjective queue backlog blocks reruns), plus decomposition of the error output path.

---

### 8. `desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_workflow.py`
**What changed:** The ~80-line inline plan-reconciliation block inside `merge_scan_results` was extracted into a dedicated `_reconcile_plan_post_scan` function, itself composed of six focused helpers (`_clear_needs_rescan_flag`, `_plan_has_user_content`, `_apply_plan_reconciliation`, `_sync_unscored_dimensions`, `_sync_stale_dimensions`, `_sync_auto_clusters`). Two new behaviors added: `_seed_plan_start_scores` and `_clear_plan_start_scores_if_queue_empty` implement a plan-lifecycle score snapshot. Deferred imports throughout are hoisted to module level. The broad `except Exception: pass` is replaced with typed `PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS`.
**Net lines:** +147 added, -79 removed (net +68)
**Category: feature addition + extracting to new module** — the decomposition is real, but so is the new plan-start-score lifecycle feature.

---

### 9. `desloppify/app/commands/show/scope.py`
**What changed:** The three branches of `resolve_entity` (special view, mechanical dimension, subjective dimension) are each extracted into `_resolve_special_view`, `_resolve_mechanical_dimension`, and `_resolve_subjective_dimension`. The deferred import of `registry_mod` is hoisted to the top.
**Net lines:** +62 added, -51 removed (net +11)
**Category: genuine refactor** — no behavior change, pure decomposition.

---

### 10. `desloppify/core/config.py`
**What changed:** Two large functions were decomposed. `set_config_value`'s four type-dispatch branches became `_set_int_config_value`, `_set_bool_config_value`, `_set_str_config_value`, `_set_list_config_value`. `_migrate_from_state_files`'s per-file loop body became `_load_state_file_payload`, `_merge_legacy_state_config`, `_strip_config_from_state_file`, `_migrate_single_state_file`.
**Net lines:** +96 added, -52 removed (net +44)
**Category: genuine refactor** — pure decomposition, no semantic change.

---

### 11. `desloppify/engine/_plan/operations.py`
**What changed:** The `_resolve_position` function's four large `if` branches were decomposed into `_resolve_relative_position` (handles `before`/`after`) and `_resolve_offset_position` (handles `up`/`down`), with a shared `_find_index` helper. A deferred import of `empty_plan` in `reset_plan` is hoisted to the module-level import. The `before`/`after` branches are merged into one using `in {"before", "after"}`.
**Net lines:** +61 added, -42 removed (net +19)
**Category: genuine refactor** — cleaner decomposition with minor consolidation, no behavior change.

---

### 12. `desloppify/engine/_plan/schema.py`
**What changed:** The large `ensure_plan_defaults` function was decomposed into three helpers: `_ensure_container_types`, `_migrate_deferred_to_skipped`, and `_normalize_cluster_defaults`. The `plan_start_scores` field is added to `PlanModel` TypedDict and to `empty_plan()` / `_ensure_container_types`. The deferred migration logic now uses early-return style.
**Net lines:** +57 added, -37 removed (net +20)
**Category: genuine refactor + feature addition** — decomposition is pure refactor; `plan_start_scores` is a new schema field supporting the lifecycle score feature.

---

### 13. `desloppify/app/output/tree_text.py`
**What changed:** The leaf-node and branch-node rendering code inside `_print_tree` were extracted into `_render_leaf_node` and `_render_branch_node`, and child-sorting logic into `_sorted_children`. Both new helpers return a `bool` indicating whether anything was rendered. `_print_tree` itself now delegates entirely to these helpers.
**Net lines:** +97 added, -39 removed (net +58)
**Category: genuine refactor** — no output behavior change, only structural decomposition.

---

### 14. `desloppify/app/output/scorecard_parts/dimensions.py`
**What changed:** `resolve_scorecard_lang`'s three data-source probe branches (scan history, capabilities, findings) were extracted into `_lang_from_scan_history`, `_lang_from_capabilities`, `_lang_from_findings`. The main function becomes a single `or`-chained expression.
**Net lines:** +56 added, -22 removed (net +34)
**Category: genuine refactor** — functionally identical, decomposed for clarity.

---

### 15. `desloppify/intelligence/review/remediation.py`
**What changed:** The module previously re-exported two symbols directly from an internal engine module. It now wraps each in a thin forwarding function that performs a deferred import of the internal symbol, making the public surface independent of the internal module's import path.
**Net lines:** +34 added, -6 removed (net +28)
**Category: extracting to new module** (lazy facade pattern) — creates an import-decoupled public API surface, adding an indirection layer.

---

### 16. `desloppify/languages/_framework/generic_parts/__init__.py`
**What changed:** The entire `__init__.py` — all imports and the `__all__` list — was deleted, replaced with a single docstring line.
**Net lines:** 0 added, -46 removed (net -46)
**Category: cleanup** — the public re-export surface of the package was intentionally removed, likely because callers now import directly from submodules.

---

## Summary Table

| File | Category | Net Lines |
|---|---|---|

| `commands/next.py` | feature addition | +61 |
| `next_parts/render.py` | feature addition | +68 |
| `plan/queue_render.py` | genuine refactor | 0 |

The dominant pattern across this changeset is **genuine refactor** (decomposing large functions into private helpers without behavioral change). The actual new behavior is concentrated in four files: `next.py`, `render.py`, `preflight.py`, and `scan_workflow.py`, which all relate to a single coherent feature: **plan-lifecycle score tracking** (freezing a start score when a plan cycle begins, displaying progress toward it, and gating review reruns on subjective queue backlog).

## Assessment of New Production Files

### helpers/finding_payloads.py
1. Typed accessors (`FindingDetail` TypedDict + helpers) for extracting lines, dimensions, and line numbers from raw finding dicts.
2. ~45 lines of actual code.

4. **Useful extraction** — consolidates a fragile pattern that was clearly duplicated, and the TypedDict gives mypy coverage.

---

### helpers/guardrails.py
1. Single function that prints the synthesis-phase guardrail warning banner, swallowing plan-load errors gracefully.
2. ~18 lines of actual code.
3. Extracted from command entrypoints that each printed this inline.
4. **Useful extraction** — small but the error-handling wrapper around plan load is worth centralizing; otherwise each caller silently drops exceptions differently.

---

### helpers/lazy_exports.py
1. Two generic helpers (`lazy_module_getattr`, `lazy_module_dir`) for implementing `__getattr__`/`__dir__` lazy-import patterns in `__init__` modules.
2. ~22 lines of actual code.
3. New abstraction to DRY up the lazy-import pattern that appears in several `__init__.py` files.
4. **Over-engineered** — the lazy-import pattern it abstracts is already 3-4 lines of code per site, and none of the `__init__` files in this changeset actually use it (they all hand-roll the pattern). It adds indirection without being adopted.

---

---

### core/io/__init__.py, core/io/api.py, core/io/impl.py
1. `__init__`: 2-line namespace shim; `api.py`: ~50 lines delegating `colorize`, `log`, `print_table`, `display_entries` to `core.output`; `impl.py`: 3-line star-import re-export of `core.output`.
2. `api.py`: ~40 lines actual; `impl.py`: 3 lines actual.
3. Extracted from `utils.py` / `core.output` to create a stable IO API surface.
4. **Over-engineered** — `api.py` is pure delegation with zero logic; `impl.py` is a 3-line star-import. This tripled the file count for `core.output` with no functional benefit. `impl.py` in particular is pointless.

---

### core/pathing/__init__.py, core/pathing/api.py, core/pathing/impl.py
1. Same pattern as `core/io`: `__init__` is a namespace shim; `api.py` (~90 lines) delegates all path functions to `file_paths`; `impl.py` is a 3-line star-import.
2. `api.py`: ~70 lines actual; `impl.py`: 3 lines actual.
3. Extracted from `utils.py` / `file_paths.py`.
4. **Over-engineered** — same verdict as `core/io`. The `DynamicPathProxy` usage in `api.py` for `DEFAULT_PATH`/`SRC_PATH` is the only thing here that isn't pure delegation, and that could have gone directly in `file_paths.py`.

---

### engine/_plan/epic_synthesis.py
1. The LLM-driven epic synthesis engine: collects findings/clusters, builds a structured prompt, parses LLM JSON output, and applies the result to mutate the plan (create/update synthesis-clusters, dismiss findings, reorder queue).
2. ~550 lines of actual code.
3. New feature — the LLM synthesis concept is entirely new.
4. **New feature** — substantial, well-structured engine module. The dataclass-based types (`SynthesisInput`, `SynthesisResult`, etc.) and the clear separation of collect/build/parse/apply is good architecture.

---

### engine/planning/queue_policy.py
1. `OpenPlanQueuePolicy` frozen dataclass + `build_open_plan_queue()` — canonical queue-building policy for plan rendering surfaces.
2. ~45 lines of actual code.
3. Extracted from plan rendering code to centralize queue-build defaults for the planning/rendering layer.
4. **Useful extraction** — similar to `app/commands/plan/queue_policy.py` but for the engine-layer rendering; consolidates `_subjective_threshold()` logic that was likely inline.

---

### languages/typescript/syntax/__init__.py and syntax/scanner.py
1. `__init__` re-exports `scan_code`; `scanner.py` is a 34-line generator that yields `(index, char, in_string)` tuples from TypeScript source, handling escape sequences.
2. `scanner.py`: ~22 lines actual.
3. `scan_code` was extracted from a TypeScript fixer or detector where string-aware scanning was inlined (replacing the `_scan_code()` pattern in `_smell_helpers.py`).
4. **Useful extraction** — string-aware character scanning is a well-defined primitive that belongs in one place; it was previously duplicated between detectors and fixers.

---

## Summary

**Genuinely useful (14):** `finding_payloads`, `guardrails`, `queue_progress`, `app/plan/queue_policy`, `synthesis_playbook`, `synthesize_handlers`, `importing/modes`, `payload/packet_builder`, `assessment_integrity`, `runtime/__init__`, `runtime/policy`, `runtime/setup`, `workflow_text`, `scan_agent_env`, `scan_preflight`, `scan_skill_sync`, `compat/utils`, `path_proxy`, `exception_sets`, `epic_synthesis`, `engine/planning/queue_policy`, `typescript/syntax/scanner`

**Empty/placeholder packages with no real content (5):** `review/importing/__init__`, `review/normalization/__init__`, `review/orchestration/__init__`, `review/payload/__init__`, `review/quality/__init__`

**Single-constant files that don't warrant their own module (2):** `orchestration/runtime_defaults.py` (1 constant), `payload/constants.py` (1 constant)

**Pure delegation layers that add indirection without value (6 files across 3 packages):** `core/discovery/api.py`, `core/io/api.py`, `core/io/impl.py`, `core/pathing/api.py`, `core/pathing/impl.py` — and the `__init__` shims for each

**Not-adopted utility:** `lazy_exports.py` — the abstraction exists but isn't used by the `__init__` modules in this changeset

The biggest concern is the `core/{discovery,io,pathing}` refactoring: it created 9 files (3 packages x 3 files each) where each `api.py` is 100% pass-through delegation with no logic. The stable-API-surface intent is good, but the same result could be achieved by just importing `file_paths`, `source_discovery`, and `output` directly, or by making those modules the stable surface.

---

## Summary: Test Reorganization Analysis

### Overall Test Count

| Category | Old HEAD | New Working Tree | Delta |
|---|---|---|---|
| All test functions (full repo) | 4,054 | ~5,540 | +~1,486 |
| But: ~728 in detectors are now DUPLICATED (explained below) | | | |
| **Effective unique tests** | **4,054** | **~4,812** | **+~758** |

The raw number went up dramatically, but that is inflated by a key issue described below.

---

### Section-by-Section Breakdown

#### 1. `tests/review/` — True Reorganization with Minor Net Growth

The 20 deleted top-level files held **449 tests**. They were redistributed into four new subdirectories:

| New Subdir | Files | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| `review/context/` | 10 files | 326 tests |
| `review/policy/` | 6 files | 91 tests |
| `review/import_scoring/` | 3 files | 30 tests |
| `review/batch/` | 5 files | 18 tests |
| **Total new** | | **465 tests** |

Every named deleted file has a matching file in the new subdirs with **identical test counts**. The net gain of +16 comes from two sources:
- `review/batch/`: three brand-new files (`test_review_batch_core`, `test_review_batches_nonfatal`, `test_review_merge`) with 9 new tests
- `review/policy/test_runtime_policy_direct.py`: 2 new tests with no old counterpart
- `test_work_queue.py` retained with 2 added tests (+2)

### Key Concerns in the Current State

1. **`tests/commands/` and `tests/detectors/` are mid-reorganization.** The old top-level files have NOT been deleted yet. If you commit as-is, pytest will run most of these tests twice (once from the old location, once from the new subdir), inflating run time and potentially causing confusing duplicate-pass output.

2. **`tests/review/` reorganization is complete and clean** — old files are deleted, new subdirs have the content, no duplication.

3. **`test_py_smells.py` split is clean** — old file is now a stub, all tests migrated to the three new files with exact count preservation (59 in, 59 out, +1 perf test).

### SUMMARY

**Critical Findings:**

1. **exception_sets is NEW on abandoned-refactor** - does not exist on main, so you MUST bring it when merging the desired modules
   
2. **Core discovery/io/pathing are HEAVILY used** - they're imported 100+ times across the codebase. These are truly "to be excluded" and code should NOT import from them.

3. **Direct imports to exclude:**
   - `desloppify.core.discovery` (only 1 direct import in compat/utils.py:18)
   - `desloppify.core.io` (3 imports in compat and synthesize_handlers)
   - `desloppify.core.pathing` (3 imports in compat and review/runtime/setup.py)
   - The `_discovery_api` and related `_api` facades are used everywhere else (which is expected and okay)

4. **Modules to bring with no external imports:**
   - finding_payloads - safe to bring
   - queue_policy - safe to bring
   - workflow_text - safe to bring
   - scan_preflight - safe to bring
   - payload - safe to bring
   - quality - safe to bring
   - importing - safe to bring

5. **Modules with dependencies needing careful handling:**
   - `queue_progress` → imports `exception_sets` (must bring together)
   - `guardrails` → imports `exception_sets` (must bring together)
   - `synthesis_playbook` → used by `synthesize_handlers` + imports `core.io.api` (problem!)
   - `scan_agent_env` → used by `scan_skill_sync`
   - `review/runtime` → has sub-modules that other code imports from
   - `review/orchestration` → has sub-modules that tests import from

```
"""Typed finding/detail accessors for command-layer renderers."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Mapping, TypedDict, cast

from desloppify import state as state_mod


class FindingDetail(TypedDict, total=False):
    """Known finding detail keys used by command rendering paths."""

    category: str
    count: int
    dimension: str
    dimension_name: str
    importers: int
    investigation: bool
    line: int
    lines: list[int]
    strict_score: float
    suggestion: str


def state_findings(
    state: state_mod.StateModel | dict[str, Any],
    *,
    strict: bool = False,
) -> dict[str, state_mod.Finding]:
    """Return typed state findings map or raise on contract violations."""
    if strict:
        return state_mod.require_findings(state)
    findings = state.get("findings")
    if isinstance(findings, dict):
        return cast(dict[str, state_mod.Finding], findings)
    return {}


def finding_detail(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> FindingDetail:
    """Return typed detail mapping for a finding-like payload."""
    detail = finding.get("detail")
    if isinstance(detail, dict):
        return cast(FindingDetail, detail)
    return FindingDetail()


def detail_dimension(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> str:
    """Return normalized detail.dimension value."""
    return str(finding_detail(finding).get("dimension", "")).strip()


def detail_lines(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> list[int]:
    """Return normalized integer line list from finding detail."""
    raw_lines = finding_detail(finding).get("lines", [])
    if not isinstance(raw_lines, list):
        return []
    return [int(line) for line in raw_lines if isinstance(line, int | float)]


def detail_target_line(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> int | None:
    """Return primary target line from detail.line or detail.lines[0]."""
    detail = finding_detail(finding)
    line = detail.get("line")
    if isinstance(line, int):
        return line
    lines = detail_lines(finding)
    return lines[0] if lines else None


__all__ = [
    "FindingDetail",
    "detail_dimension",
    "detail_lines",
    "detail_target_line",
    "finding_detail",
    "state_findings",
]
```

```
"""Shared synthesis guardrail banner helpers for command entrypoints."""

from __future__ import annotations

import sys

from desloppify.core.exception_sets import PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS
from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
from desloppify.engine.plan import load_plan, synthesis_phase_banner


def print_synthesis_guardrail_banner(*, plan: dict | None = None) -> None:
    """Print synthesis phase guardrail banner with visible load-failure warnings."""
    try:
        resolved_plan = plan if isinstance(plan, dict) else load_plan()
        banner = synthesis_phase_banner(resolved_plan)
    except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
        print(
            colorize(
                "  Warning: synthesis guardrail unavailable "
                f"({exc.__class__.__name__}: {exc}).",
                "yellow",
            ),
            file=sys.stderr,
        )
        return
    if banner:
        print(colorize(f"  {banner}", "yellow"))


__all__ = ["print_synthesis_guardrail_banner"]
```

```
"""Handler for ``plan synthesize`` subcommand.

Implements a staged workflow: OBSERVE → REFLECT → ORGANIZE → COMMIT
with a fast-track CONFIRM-EXISTING skip path.

Stage gates validate **plan data enrichment**, not text reports:
- OBSERVE: lightweight — write an analysis of themes/root causes (100 char min)
- REFLECT: compare current findings against completed work (recurring patterns)
- ORGANIZE: structural — each manual cluster must have description + action_steps
- COMMIT: strategy must be substantive (200 char min) or "same"
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
from collections import defaultdict

from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
from desloppify.app.commands.plan.synthesis_playbook import (
    SYNTHESIS_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES,
    SYNTHESIS_STAGE_LABELS,
    SYNTH_CMD_CLUSTER_ADD,
    SYNTH_CMD_CLUSTER_CREATE,
    SYNTH_CMD_CLUSTER_ENRICH_COMPACT,
    SYNTH_CMD_CLUSTER_STEPS,
    SYNTH_CMD_COMPLETE_VERBOSE,
    SYNTH_CMD_CONFIRM_EXISTING,
    SYNTH_CMD_OBSERVE,
    SYNTH_CMD_ORGANIZE,
    SYNTH_CMD_REFLECT,
)
from desloppify.core.io.api import colorize
from desloppify.engine._plan.epic_synthesis import (
    build_synthesis_prompt,
    collect_synthesis_input,
    detect_recurring_patterns,
    extract_finding_citations,
)
from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import review_finding_snapshot_hash
from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
from desloppify.engine.plan import SYNTHESIS_ID, load_plan, save_plan


def _short_id(fid: str) -> str:
    """Extract the 8-char hash suffix from a finding ID for compact display.

    Finding IDs look like ``review::.::holistic::dim::identifier::abcdef12``.
    Commands accept the hash suffix as a shorthand for the full ID.
    """
    if "::" in fid:
        suffix = fid.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
        if len(suffix) >= 8:
            return suffix[:8]
    return fid


def _synthesis_coverage(plan: dict) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for synthesis progress."""
    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
    for c in clusters.values():
        all_cluster_ids.update(c.get("finding_ids", []))
    queue_ids = [fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", []) if fid != SYNTHESIS_ID]
    organized = sum(1 for fid in queue_ids if fid in all_cluster_ids)
    return organized, len(queue_ids), clusters


def _unenriched_clusters(plan: dict) -> list[tuple[str, list[str]]]:
    """Return clusters with findings that are missing required enrichment.

    Returns list of (cluster_name, list_of_missing_fields).
    Only checks non-auto clusters (manual clusters the agent creates).
    """
    gaps: list[tuple[str, list[str]]] = []
    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
        if not cluster.get("finding_ids"):
            continue
        if cluster.get("auto"):
            continue
```

```
"""Agent-environment detection helpers for scan reporting."""

from __future__ import annotations

import os


def is_agent_environment() -> bool:
    """Return True when scan is running inside a supported AI agent runtime."""
    return bool(
        os.environ.get("CLAUDE_CODE")
        or os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_AGENT")
        or os.environ.get("GEMINI_CLI")
        or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED")
        or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX")
        or os.environ.get("CURSOR_TRACE_ID")
    )


def detect_agent_interface() -> str | None:
    """Resolve current interface key used by `update-skill`."""
    if os.environ.get("CLAUDE_CODE"):
        return "claude"
    if os.environ.get("GEMINI_CLI"):
        return "gemini"
    if os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED") or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX"):
        return "codex"
    if os.environ.get("CURSOR_TRACE_ID"):
        return "cursor"
    return None


__all__ = ["detect_agent_interface", "is_agent_environment"]
```

```
"""Skill auto-install/update helpers for scan workflows."""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging

from desloppify.app.commands import update_skill as update_skill_mod
from desloppify.app.commands.scan.scan_agent_env import (
    detect_agent_interface,
    is_agent_environment,
)
from desloppify.core import skill_docs as skill_docs_mod

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


def _try_auto_update_skill() -> None:
    """Attempt to auto-install or update the skill document best-effort."""
    install = skill_docs_mod.find_installed_skill()
    if install and not install.stale:
        return

    try:
        interface = (
            update_skill_mod.resolve_interface(install=install)
            if install
            else detect_agent_interface()
        )
        if interface:
            update_skill_mod.update_installed_skill(interface)
    except (ImportError, OSError, RuntimeError, ValueError) as exc:
        logger.debug("Skill auto-update skipped: %s", exc, exc_info=True)
        return


def auto_update_skill() -> None:
    """Auto-install or update the skill document if an agent runtime is detected."""
    if not is_agent_environment():
        return

    _try_auto_update_skill()
    install = skill_docs_mod.find_installed_skill()
    if not install:
        names = ", ".join(sorted(skill_docs_mod.SKILL_TARGETS))
        print(
            f"No skill document found. Install one for better workflow guidance: "
            f"desloppify update-skill <{names}>"
        )
    elif install.stale:
        print(
            f"Skill document is outdated "
            f"(v{install.version}, current v{skill_docs_mod.SKILL_VERSION}). "
            f"Run: desloppify update-skill"
        )


__all__ = ["auto_update_skill"]
```

```
"""Codebase treemap visualization with HTML output and LLM-readable tree text."""

import argparse
import json
import logging
import sys
from collections import defaultdict
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from desloppify.app.output._viz_cmd_context import load_cmd_context
from desloppify.app.output.tree_text import render_tree_lines
from desloppify.core.file_paths import resolve_scan_file
from desloppify.core.fallbacks import (
    log_best_effort_failure,
    print_write_error,
    warn_best_effort,
)
from desloppify.core.output_contract import OutputResult
from desloppify.core.discovery_api import find_source_files, rel, safe_write_text
from desloppify.state import get_objective_score, get_overall_score, get_strict_score
from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
from desloppify.languages import (
    auto_detect_lang,
    available_langs,
    get_lang,
)

D3_CDN_URL = "https://d3js.org/d3.v7.min.js"
logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
_RECOVERABLE_LANG_RESOLUTION_ERRORS = (
    ImportError,
    ValueError,
    TypeError,
    AttributeError,
    OSError,
    RuntimeError,
)


__all__ = ["D3_CDN_URL", "cmd_viz", "cmd_tree"]


def _resolve_visualization_lang(path: Path, lang):
    """Resolve language config for visualization if not already provided."""
    if lang:
        return lang

    search_roots = [path if path.is_dir() else path.parent]
    search_roots.extend(search_roots[0].parents)
    warned = False
    for root in search_roots:
        try:
            detected = auto_detect_lang(root)
        except _RECOVERABLE_LANG_RESOLUTION_ERRORS as exc:
            log_best_effort_failure(
                logger,
                f"auto-detect visualization language for {root}",
                exc,
            )
            if not warned:
                warned = True
                warn_best_effort(
                    "Could not auto-detect language plugins for visualization; "
                    f"using fallback source discovery ({type(exc).__name__}: {exc})."
                )
            continue
        if detected:
            try:
                return get_lang(detected)
            except _RECOVERABLE_LANG_RESOLUTION_ERRORS as exc:
                log_best_effort_failure(
                    logger,
                    f"load visualization language plugin '{detected}'",
                    exc,
                )
                if not warned:
                    warned = True
                    warn_best_effort(
                        "Visualization language plugin failed to load; using fallback source discovery "
                        f"({type(exc).__name__}: {exc})."
                    )
                continue
    return None


def _fallback_source_files(path: Path) -> list[str]:
    """Collect source files using extensions from all registered language plugins."""
    extensions: set[str] = set()
    warned = False
    for lang_name in available_langs():
        try:
            cfg = get_lang(lang_name)
        except _RECOVERABLE_LANG_RESOLUTION_ERRORS as exc:
            log_best_effort_failure(
                logger,
                f"load fallback visualization language plugin '{lang_name}'",
                exc,
            )
            if not warned:
                warned = True
                warn_best_effort(
                    "Some language plugins could not be loaded for visualization fallback; using available plugins only "
                    f"({type(exc).__name__}: {exc})."
                )
            continue
        extensions.update(cfg.extensions)
    if not extensions:
        return []
    return find_source_files(path, sorted(extensions))


def _collect_file_data(path: Path, lang=None) -> list[dict]:
    """Collect LOC for all source files using the language's file finder."""
    resolved_lang = _resolve_visualization_lang(path, lang)
    if resolved_lang and resolved_lang.file_finder:
        source_files = resolved_lang.file_finder(path)
    else:
        source_files = _fallback_source_files(path)
    files = []
    warned_read_failure = False
    for filepath in source_files:
        try:
            p = resolve_scan_file(filepath, scan_root=path)
            content = p.read_text()
            loc = len(content.splitlines())
            files.append(
                {
                    "path": rel(filepath),
                    "abs_path": str(p.resolve()),
                    "loc": loc,
                }
            )
        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
            log_best_effort_failure(
                logger, f"read visualization source file {filepath}", exc
            )
            if not warned_read_failure:
                warned_read_failure = True
                warn_best_effort(
                    "Some visualization source files could not be read; output may be incomplete."
                )
            continue
    return files


def _build_tree(files: list[dict], dep_graph: dict, findings_by_file: dict) -> dict:
    """Build nested tree structure for D3 treemap."""
    root: dict = {"name": "src", "children": {}}

    for f in files:
        parts = f["path"].split("/")
        # Skip leading 'src/' since root is already 'src'
        if parts and parts[0] == "src":
            parts = parts[1:]
        node = root
        for part in parts[:-1]:
            if part not in node["children"]:
                node["children"][part] = {"name": part, "children": {}}
            node = node["children"][part]

        filename = parts[-1]
        resolved = f["abs_path"]
        dep_entry = dep_graph.get(resolved, {"import_count": 0, "importer_count": 0})
        file_findings = findings_by_file.get(f["path"], [])
        open_findings = [ff for ff in file_findings if ff.get("status") == "open"]

        node["children"][filename] = {
            "name": filename,
            "path": f["path"],
            "loc": max(f["loc"], 1),  # D3 needs >0 values
            "fan_in": dep_entry.get("importer_count", 0),
            "fan_out": dep_entry.get("import_count", 0),
            "findings_total": len(file_findings),
            "findings_open": len(open_findings),
            "finding_summaries": [ff.get("summary", "") for ff in open_findings[:20]],
        }

    # Convert children dicts to arrays (D3 format)
    def to_array(node: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        if "children" in node and isinstance(node["children"], dict):
            children = list(node["children"].values())
            for child in children:
                to_array(child)
            node["children"] = children
            # Remove empty directories
            node["children"] = [
                c
                for c in node["children"]
                if "loc" in c or ("children" in c and c["children"])
            ]

    to_array(root)
    return root


def _build_dep_graph_for_path(path: Path, lang) -> dict:
    """Build dependency graph using the resolved language plugin."""
    resolved_lang = _resolve_visualization_lang(path, lang)
    if resolved_lang and resolved_lang.build_dep_graph:
        try:
            return resolved_lang.build_dep_graph(path)
        except (
            OSError,
            UnicodeDecodeError,
            ValueError,
            RuntimeError,
            TypeError,
        ) as exc:
            log_best_effort_failure(logger, "build visualization dependency graph", exc)
            warn_best_effort(
                "Could not build visualization dependency graph; showing file-only view."
            )
    return {}


def _findings_by_file(state: dict | None) -> dict[str, list]:
    """Group findings from state by file path."""
    result: dict[str, list] = defaultdict(list)
    if state and state.get("findings"):
        for f in state["findings"].values():
            result[f["file"]].append(f)
    return result


def _write_visualization_output(output: Path, html: str) -> OutputResult:
    """Write visualization HTML to disk using the shared output-result contract."""
    try:
        safe_write_text(output, html)
    except OSError as exc:
        return OutputResult(
            ok=False,
            status="error",
            message=str(exc),
            error_kind="visualization_write_error",
        )
    return OutputResult(ok=True, status="written", message=f"wrote {output}")


def generate_visualization(
    path: Path, state: dict | None = None, output: Path | None = None, lang=None
) -> tuple[str, OutputResult]:
    """Generate an HTML treemap visualization and explicit output result."""
    try:
        files = _collect_file_data(path, lang)
        dep_graph = _build_dep_graph_for_path(path, lang)
        findings_by_file = _findings_by_file(state)
        tree = _build_tree(files, dep_graph, findings_by_file)
        # Escape </ to prevent </script> in filenames from breaking HTML
        tree_json = json.dumps(tree).replace("</", r"<\/")

        # Stats for header
        total_files = len(files)
        total_loc = sum(f["loc"] for f in files)
        total_findings = sum(len(v) for v in findings_by_file.values())
        open_findings = sum(
            1 for fs in findings_by_file.values() for f in fs if f.get("status") == "open"
        )
        if state:
            overall_score = get_overall_score(state)
            objective_score = get_objective_score(state)
            strict_score = get_strict_score(state)
        else:
            overall_score = objective_score = strict_score = None

        def fmt_score(value):
            return f"{value:.1f}" if isinstance(value, int | float) else "N/A"

        replacements = {
            "__D3_CDN_URL__": D3_CDN_URL,
            "__TREE_DATA__": tree_json,
            "__TOTAL_FILES__": str(total_files),
            "__TOTAL_LOC__": f"{total_loc:,}",
            "__TOTAL_FINDINGS__": str(total_findings),
            "__OPEN_FINDINGS__": str(open_findings),
            "__OVERALL_SCORE__": fmt_score(overall_score),
            "__OBJECTIVE_SCORE__": fmt_score(objective_score),
            "__STRICT_SCORE__": fmt_score(strict_score),
        }
        html = _get_html_template()
        for placeholder, value in replacements.items():
            html = html.replace(placeholder, value)
    except OSError as exc:
        return "", OutputResult(
            ok=False,
            status="error",
            message=str(exc),
            error_kind="visualization_generation_error",
        )

    if output:
        write_result = _write_visualization_output(output, html)
        if not write_result.ok:
            message = write_result.message or "unknown write failure"
            print_write_error(output, OSError(message), label="visualization")
            return html, write_result
        return html, write_result

    return html, OutputResult(
        ok=True,
        status="not_requested",
        message="visualization generated in memory only",
    )


def cmd_viz(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Generate HTML treemap visualization."""
    path, lang, state = load_cmd_context(args)
    output = Path(getattr(args, "output", None) or ".desloppify/treemap.html")
    print(colorize("Collecting file data and building dependency graph...", "dim"))
    _, output_result = generate_visualization(path, state, output, lang=lang)
    if output_result.status != "written":
        message = output_result.message or "unknown write failure"
        print(
            colorize(
                f"\nVisualization write failed ({output_result.status}): {output} ({message})",
                "red",
            ),
            file=sys.stderr,
        )
        raise SystemExit(1)
    print(colorize(f"\nTreemap written to {output}", "green"))
    print(colorize(f"Open in browser: file://{output.resolve()}", "dim"))

@dataclass
class TreeTextOptions:
    """Text tree rendering options."""
    max_depth: int = 2
    focus: str | None = None
    min_loc: int = 0
    sort_by: str = "loc"
    detail: bool = False


def generate_tree_text(
    path: Path,
    state: dict | None = None,
    options: TreeTextOptions | None = None,
    *,
    lang=None,
) -> str:
    """Generate text-based annotated tree of the codebase."""
    resolved_options = options or TreeTextOptions()
    files = _collect_file_data(path, lang)
    dep_graph = _build_dep_graph_for_path(path, lang)
    tree = _build_tree(files, dep_graph, _findings_by_file(state))

    root = tree
    if resolved_options.focus:
        parts = resolved_options.focus.strip("/").split("/")
        if parts and parts[0] == "src":
            parts = parts[1:]
        for part in parts:
            found = None
            for child in root.get("children", []):
                if child["name"] == part:
                    found = child
                    break
            if found is None:
                return f"Directory not found: {resolved_options.focus}"
            root = found

    lines = render_tree_lines(
        root,
        max_depth=resolved_options.max_depth,
        min_loc=resolved_options.min_loc,
        sort_by=resolved_options.sort_by,
        detail=resolved_options.detail,
    )
    return "\n".join(lines)


def cmd_tree(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Print annotated codebase tree to terminal."""
    path, lang, state = load_cmd_context(args)
    print(
        generate_tree_text(
            path,
            state,
            options=TreeTextOptions(
                max_depth=getattr(args, "depth", 2),
                focus=getattr(args, "focus", None),
                min_loc=getattr(args, "min_loc", 0),
                sort_by=getattr(args, "sort", "loc"),
                detail=getattr(args, "detail", False),
            ),
            lang=lang,
        )
    )


def _get_html_template() -> str:
    """Read the HTML treemap template from the external file."""
    return (Path(__file__).parent / "_viz_template.html").read_text()
```

```
    """Return one normalized subjective follow-up command string."""

def next_subjective_command_text(next_command: str | None) -> str:

def claude_durable_path_text(*, submit_command_hint: str = "--external-submit") -> str:

def findings_only_fallback_text(
    *,
    include_prepare_step: bool = False,
    include_scan_after_import: bool = False,
) -> str:
    """Render one shared findings-only fallback guidance line."""
    import_cmd = "desloppify review --import findings.json"

    if include_prepare_step:
        return (

        '5. Emergency only: `--manual-override --attest "<why>"` (provisional; expires on next scan)',
    )

        f"6. {findings_only_fallback_text(include_scan_after_import=True)}",
    )
```

```
"""Epic synthesis engine — LLM-driven meta-plan for review findings.

Clusters review findings by root cause, dismisses false positives,
resolves contradictions, and orders by dependency. The synthesis is incremental:
it updates existing synthesis-clusters rather than recreating from scratch.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import logging
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any

from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
    EPIC_PREFIX,
    VALID_EPIC_DIRECTIONS,
    Cluster,
    PlanModel,
    ensure_plan_defaults,
    synthesis_clusters,
)
from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import (
    SYNTHESIS_ID,
    review_finding_snapshot_hash,
)
from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel, utc_now

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Finding-ID citation extraction
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

FINDING_ID_RE = re.compile(r"[a-z_]+::[a-f0-9]{8,}")


def extract_finding_citations(text: str, valid_ids: set[str]) -> set[str]:
    """Extract finding IDs cited in free text.

    Matches full finding IDs (e.g. ``review::abcdef12``) or bare 8+ char
    hex suffixes that correspond to a known finding.
    """
    cited: set[str] = set()
    # Match full finding IDs
    for match in FINDING_ID_RE.finditer(text):
        candidate = match.group()
        if candidate in valid_ids:
            cited.add(candidate)
    # Match 8+ char hex suffixes
    for token in re.findall(r"[0-9a-f]{8,}", text):
        for valid_id in valid_ids:
            if valid_id.endswith("::" + token):
                cited.add(valid_id)
                break
    return cited
```

```
"""Review import-mode package."""
"""Stable review import mode normalization for command entrypoints."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Literal

ReviewImportMode = Literal[
    "auto",
    "trusted_internal",
    "attested_external",
    "manual_override",
]

_ALLOWED_MODES: set[str] = {
    "auto",
    "trusted_internal",
    "attested_external",
    "manual_override",
}


def normalize_review_import_mode(raw: str | None) -> ReviewImportMode:
    """Normalize user/caller import mode text to one stable mode token."""
    mode = (raw or "auto").strip().lower().replace("-", "_")
    if mode not in _ALLOWED_MODES:
        allowed = ", ".join(sorted(_ALLOWED_MODES))
        raise ValueError(f"invalid review import mode '{raw}'; expected one of: {allowed}")
    return mode  # type: ignore[return-value]


def _validate_mode_compatibility(
    *,
    mode: ReviewImportMode,
    attested: bool,
    manual: bool,
) -> None:
    if mode == "trusted_internal" and (attested or manual):
        raise ValueError(
            "import_mode=trusted_internal cannot be combined with attested/manual flags"
        )
    if mode == "attested_external" and manual:
        raise ValueError(
            "import_mode=attested_external cannot be combined with manual override flags"
        )
    if mode == "manual_override" and attested:
        raise ValueError(
            "import_mode=manual_override cannot be combined with attested external flags"
        )
```

```
def redacted_review_config(config: dict[str, object] | None) -> dict[str, object]:
    """Return review config payload with score-target hints removed."""
    if not isinstance(config, dict):
        return {}
    return {key: value for key, value in config.items() if key != "target_strict_score"}


def coerce_review_batch_file_limit(config: dict[str, object] | None) -> int | None:
    """Resolve per-batch review file cap from config (0/negative => unlimited)."""
    raw = (config or {}).get("review_batch_max_files", DEFAULT_REVIEW_BATCH_MAX_FILES)
    try:
        value = int(raw)
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return DEFAULT_REVIEW_BATCH_MAX_FILES
    return value if value > 0 else None

    if include_scan_after_import:
        command += " --scan-after-import"
```

```
"""Quality guardrails and integrity checks for review workflows."""
"""Shared subjective-assessment integrity helpers for review import flows."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Callable


def subjective_at_target_dimensions(
    state_or_dim_scores: dict,
    dim_scores: dict | None = None,
    *,
    target: float,
    scorecard_subjective_entries_fn,
    matches_target_score_fn,
) -> list[dict]:
    """Return scorecard-aligned subjective rows that sit on the target threshold."""
    state = state_or_dim_scores
    if dim_scores is None:
        dim_scores = state_or_dim_scores
        state = {"dimension_scores": dim_scores}

    rows: list[dict] = []
    for entry in scorecard_subjective_entries_fn(state, dim_scores=dim_scores):
        if entry.get("placeholder"):
            continue
        strict_val = float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))
        if matches_target_score_fn(strict_val, target):
            rows.append(
                {
                    "name": str(entry.get("name", "Subjective")),
                    "score": strict_val,
                    "cli_keys": list(entry.get("cli_keys", [])),
                }
            )
    rows.sort(key=lambda item: item["name"].lower())
    return rows


def bind_scorecard_subjective_at_target(
    *,
    scorecard_subjective_entries_fn=None,
    reporting_dimensions_mod=None,
    subjective_integrity_mod,
) -> Callable[..., list[dict]]:
    """Bind scorecard-specific dependencies once for import/output call sites.

    ``reporting_dimensions_mod`` is kept for compatibility; new callers should
    pass ``scorecard_subjective_entries_fn`` explicitly.
    """
    if scorecard_subjective_entries_fn is None and reporting_dimensions_mod is not None:
        scorecard_subjective_entries_fn = (
            reporting_dimensions_mod.scorecard_subjective_entries
        )
    if not callable(scorecard_subjective_entries_fn):
        raise ValueError("scorecard_subjective_entries_fn must be callable")

    def bound(
        state_or_dim_scores: dict[str, object],
        dim_scores: dict[str, object] | None = None,
        *,
        target: float,
    ) -> list[dict]:
        return subjective_at_target_dimensions(
            state_or_dim_scores,
            dim_scores,
            target=target,
            scorecard_subjective_entries_fn=scorecard_subjective_entries_fn,
            matches_target_score_fn=subjective_integrity_mod.matches_target_score,
        )

    return bound


__all__ = [
    "bind_scorecard_subjective_at_target",
    "subjective_at_target_dimensions",
]
```

```
"""Runtime helpers for review command orchestration."""

import importlib

from desloppify.app.commands.review.runtime.setup import (
    setup_lang,
    setup_lang_concrete,
)

_LAZY_EXPORTS = {
    "runner_helpers": "desloppify.app.commands.review.runner_helpers",
    "policy": ".policy",
}

__all__ = ["setup_lang", "setup_lang_concrete", *_LAZY_EXPORTS]


def __getattr__(name: str):
    target = _LAZY_EXPORTS.get(name)
    if target is None:
        raise AttributeError(f"module {__name__!r} has no attribute {name!r}")
    if target.startswith("."):
        value = importlib.import_module(target, __name__)
    else:
        value = importlib.import_module(target)
    globals()[name] = value
    return value


def __dir__() -> list[str]:
    return sorted(set(globals()) | set(__all__))
```

```
"""Shared queue-construction policy for next/plan command surfaces."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

from desloppify.core.enums import Status, canonical_status_filter
from desloppify.engine.work_queue import QueueBuildOptions, build_work_queue


def build_plan_queue(
    state: dict[str, Any],
    *,
    tier: int | None = None,
    count: int | None = None,
    scope: str | None = None,
    status: str = Status.OPEN.value,
    target_strict: float = 95.0,
    no_tier_fallback: bool = False,
    explain: bool = False,
    plan: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
    include_skipped: bool = False,
    cluster: str | None = None,
    collapse_clusters: bool = False,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Build one queue with consistent policy defaults used by CLI surfaces."""
    normalized_status = canonical_status_filter(status, default=Status.OPEN.value)
    return build_work_queue(
        state,
        options=QueueBuildOptions(
            tier=tier,
            count=count,
            scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
            scope=scope,
            status=normalized_status,
            include_subjective=True,
            subjective_threshold=target_strict,
            no_tier_fallback=no_tier_fallback,
            explain=explain,
            plan=plan,
            include_skipped=include_skipped,
            cluster=cluster,
            collapse_clusters=collapse_clusters,
        ),
    )


__all__ = ["build_plan_queue"]
```

```
"""Focused tests for review batch-core payload extraction."""

from __future__ import annotations

from desloppify.app.commands.review import batch_core as batch_core_mod


def test_extract_json_payload_prefers_richer_candidate() -> None:
    raw = """
runner chatter
{"assessments":{"design_coherence":70},"findings":[]}
more chatter
{"assessments":{"design_coherence":71,"logic_clarity":69},"findings":[{"identifier":"x"}],"dimension_notes":{"design_coherence":{"evidence":["a"],"impact_scope":"subsystem","fix_scope":"single_file","confidence":"medium","issues_preventing_higher_score":"n/a"}}}
"""
    payload = batch_core_mod.extract_json_payload(raw, log_fn=lambda _msg: None)
    assert isinstance(payload, dict)
    assessments = payload.get("assessments")
    assert isinstance(assessments, dict)
    assert set(assessments) == {"design_coherence", "logic_clarity"}
    findings = payload.get("findings")
```

```
"""Central command registry for CLI command handler resolution."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Callable
from typing import Any

CommandHandler = Callable[[Any], None]

_COMMAND_HANDLERS: dict[str, CommandHandler] | None = None


def _build_handlers() -> dict[str, CommandHandler]:
    """Import all command modules and build the handler dict on first access."""
    from desloppify.app.commands.config_cmd import cmd_config
    from desloppify.app.commands.detect import cmd_detect
    from desloppify.app.commands.dev_cmd import cmd_dev
    from desloppify.app.commands.exclude_cmd import cmd_exclude
    from desloppify.app.commands.fix.cmd import cmd_fix
    from desloppify.app.commands.langs import cmd_langs
    from desloppify.app.commands.move.move import cmd_move
    from desloppify.app.commands.next import cmd_next
    from desloppify.app.commands.plan.cmd import cmd_plan
    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd import cmd_ignore_pattern
    from desloppify.app.commands.review import cmd_review
    from desloppify.app.commands.scan import cmd_scan
    from desloppify.app.commands.show.cmd import cmd_show
    from desloppify.app.commands.status_cmd import cmd_status
    from desloppify.app.commands.update_skill import cmd_update_skill
    from desloppify.app.commands.viz_cmd import cmd_tree, cmd_viz
    from desloppify.app.commands.zone_cmd import cmd_zone

    return {
        "scan": cmd_scan,
        "status": cmd_status,
        "show": cmd_show,
        "next": cmd_next,
        "ignore": cmd_ignore_pattern,
        "exclude": cmd_exclude,
        "fix": cmd_fix,
        "plan": cmd_plan,
        "detect": cmd_detect,
        "tree": cmd_tree,
        "viz": cmd_viz,
        "move": cmd_move,
        "zone": cmd_zone,
        "review": cmd_review,
        "config": cmd_config,
        "dev": cmd_dev,
        "langs": cmd_langs,
        "update-skill": cmd_update_skill,
    }


def get_command_handlers() -> dict[str, CommandHandler]:
    """Return cached command handler dict, building on first access."""
    global _COMMAND_HANDLERS
    if _COMMAND_HANDLERS is None:
        _COMMAND_HANDLERS = _build_handlers()
    return _COMMAND_HANDLERS


__all__ = ["CommandHandler", "get_command_handlers"]
```

```
"""plan command: dispatcher for plan subcommands."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse

from desloppify.app.commands.plan.cluster_handlers import cmd_cluster_dispatch
from desloppify.app.commands.plan.move_handlers import cmd_plan_move
from desloppify.app.commands.plan.override_handlers import (
    cmd_plan_describe,
    cmd_plan_done,
    cmd_plan_focus,
    cmd_plan_note,
    cmd_plan_reopen,
    cmd_plan_skip,
    cmd_plan_unskip,
)
from desloppify.app.commands.plan.queue_render import cmd_plan_queue
from desloppify.app.commands.plan_cmd import cmd_plan_output
from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
from desloppify.engine.plan import load_plan, reset_plan, save_plan


def _cmd_plan_generate(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Generate the prioritized markdown plan (existing behavior)."""
    cmd_plan_output(args)


def _cmd_plan_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Show plan metadata summary."""
    plan = load_plan()
    ordered = len(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
    total_skipped = len(skipped)
    temp_count = sum(1 for e in skipped.values() if e.get("kind") == "temporary")
    perm_count = sum(1 for e in skipped.values() if e.get("kind") == "permanent")
    fp_count = sum(1 for e in skipped.values() if e.get("kind") == "false_positive")
    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    overrides = plan.get("overrides", {})
    active = plan.get("active_cluster")
    superseded = len(plan.get("superseded", {}))

    # Count meaningful annotations (descriptions and notes)
    described = sum(1 for o in overrides.values() if o.get("description"))
    noted = sum(1 for o in overrides.values() if o.get("note"))

    print(colorize("  Living Plan Status", "bold"))
    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 40, "dim"))
    print(f"  Queue:            {ordered} items prioritized")
    if total_skipped:
        print(f"  Skipped:          {total_skipped} (temp: {temp_count}, wontfix: {perm_count}, fp: {fp_count})")
    else:
        print(f"  Skipped:          0")
    print(f"  Clusters:         {len(clusters)}")
    if clusters:
        for name, cluster in clusters.items():
            desc = cluster.get("description") or ""
            member_count = len(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
            marker = " (focused)" if name == active else ""
            desc_str = f" — {desc}" if desc else ""
            print(f"    {name}: {member_count} items{desc_str}{marker}")
    if described or noted:
        print(f"  Annotations:      {described} described, {noted} noted")
    if active:
        print(f"  Focus:            {active}")
    if superseded:
        print(f"  Disappeared:      {superseded} (resolved or removed since last scan)")


def _cmd_plan_reset(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Reset the plan to empty."""
    plan = load_plan()
    reset_plan(plan)
    save_plan(plan)
    print(colorize("  Plan reset to empty.", "green"))


def cmd_plan(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Dispatch plan subcommand or generate markdown output."""
    plan_action = getattr(args, "plan_action", None)

    if plan_action is None:
        _cmd_plan_generate(args)
        return

    if plan_action == "show":
        _cmd_plan_show(args)
        return

    if plan_action == "queue":
        cmd_plan_queue(args)
        return

    if plan_action == "reset":
        _cmd_plan_reset(args)
        return

    if plan_action == "move":
        cmd_plan_move(args)
        return

    if plan_action in (
        "describe", "note", "focus",
        "skip", "unskip", "reopen", "done",
    ):
        dispatch = {
            "describe": cmd_plan_describe,
            "done": cmd_plan_done,
            "note": cmd_plan_note,
            "focus": cmd_plan_focus,
            "skip": cmd_plan_skip,
            "unskip": cmd_plan_unskip,
            "reopen": cmd_plan_reopen,
        }
        dispatch[plan_action](args)
        return

    if plan_action == "cluster":
        cmd_cluster_dispatch(args)
        return

    print(f"Unknown plan action: {plan_action}")


# Backwards-compatible alias
cmd_plan_output = cmd_plan

__all__ = ["cmd_plan", "cmd_plan_output"]
```

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Desloppify**: Python CLI tool for codebase health tracking with dimension-based scoring
   - **Score formula**: `overall = 40% mechanical + 60% subjective` — subjective reviews dominate the score
   - **Plan lifecycle**: Queue of findings to work through; score is frozen at plan start until queue is cleared
   - **Scan preflight gate**: New feature that blocks re-scanning when queue items remain (requires `--force-rescan --attest "..."`)
   - **State persistence**: `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` files, plan in `.desloppify/plan.json`
   - **Queue workflow**: `next` → work item → `plan done` (resolve) or `plan skip` (defer) → repeat → `scan` when clear
   - **Excluded over-engineering patterns**: Pure delegation layers (`core/{discovery,io,pathing}`), single-constant packages, empty `__init__.py` placeholder packages, unused utility files

I need to understand why `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` properly imports assessment scores as durable, but when we manually merge the same raw batch results and import them with `--import merged.json`, the scores are flagged as "provisional" and will reset on next scan.

The issue: we ran the review command in the background, all 12 codex batches completed successfully with raw result files, but the broken pipe (from running in background) killed the merge/import step. Now we have all the results but can't import them durably.

Investigate the review import code path to understand:
1. What provenance/trust mechanism makes `--run-batches` imports "durable" vs manual imports "provisional"?
2. Where in the code does it check for this trust/provenance?
3. What would it take to either:
   a. Add a `--import-run <run-dir>` command that replays the merge/import from existing batch results
   b. Or fix the provenance issue so manual imports of valid codex results are trusted

```
    40→class ImportFlagValidationError(ValueError):
    41→    """Raised when review import CLI flags are mutually incompatible."""
    42→
    43→
    44→def _validate_import_flag_combos(
    45→    *,
    46→    attested_external: bool,
    47→    allow_partial: bool,
    48→    override_enabled: bool,
    49→    override_attest: str | None,
    50→) -> None:
    51→    """Fail fast on conflicting import flags to keep behavior explicit."""
    52→    if attested_external and override_enabled:
    53→        raise ImportFlagValidationError(
    54→            "--attested-external cannot be combined with --manual-override"
    55→        )
    56→    if attested_external and allow_partial:
    57→        raise ImportFlagValidationError(
    58→            "--attested-external cannot be combined with --allow-partial"
    59→        )
    60→    if override_enabled and allow_partial:
    61→        raise ImportFlagValidationError(
    62→            "--manual-override cannot be combined with --allow-partial"
    63→        )
    64→    if override_enabled and (
    65→        not isinstance(override_attest, str) or not override_attest.strip()
    66→    ):
    67→        raise ImportFlagValidationError("--manual-override requires --attest")
    68→

    70→def _imported_assessment_keys(findings_data: ReviewImportPayload) -> set[str]:
    71→    """Return normalized assessment dimension keys from payload."""
    72→    raw_assessments = findings_data["assessments"]
    73→    keys: set[str] = set()
    74→    for raw_key in raw_assessments:
    75→        normalized = normalize_dimension_name(str(raw_key))
    76→        if normalized:
    77→            keys.add(normalized)
    78→    return keys
    79→

    81→def _mark_manual_override_assessments_provisional(
    82→    state: dict,
    83→    *,
    84→    assessment_keys: set[str],
    85→) -> int:
    86→    """Mark imported manual override assessments as provisional until next scan."""
    87→    if not assessment_keys:
    88→        return 0
    89→    store = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    90→    if not isinstance(store, dict):
    91→        return 0
    92→
    93→    now = state_mod.utc_now()
    94→    expires_scan = int(state.get("scan_count", 0) or 0) + 1
    95→    marked = 0
    96→    for key in sorted(assessment_keys):
    97→        payload = store.get(key)
    98→        if not isinstance(payload, dict):
    99→            continue
   100→        payload["source"] = "manual_override"
   101→        payload["assessed_at"] = now
   102→        payload["provisional_override"] = True
   103→        payload["provisional_until_scan"] = expires_scan
   104→        payload.pop("placeholder", None)
   105→        marked += 1
   106→    return marked
   107→
   108→
   109→def _clear_provisional_override_flags(
   110→    state: dict,
   111→    *,
   112→    assessment_keys: set[str],
   113→) -> int:
   114→    """Clear provisional override flags when trusted internal assessments replace them."""

   121→    cleared = 0

   126→        if payload.pop("provisional_override", None) is not None:

   128→        payload.pop("provisional_until_scan", None)
   129→        if payload.get("source") == "manual_override":
   130→            payload["source"] = "holistic"
   131→    return cleared
```
